- run_id: `20260704_231922_193838_2`

- query: Use MATHS rigor= formal to solve this: A line in the plane is called \\emph{sunny} if it is not parallel to any of the $x$-axis, the $y$-axis, or the line $x+y=0$.

Let $n \ge 3$ be a given integer. Determine all nonnegative integers $k$ such that there exist $n$ distinct lines in the plane satisfying both of the following:
\begin{itemize}
\item for all positive integers $a$ and $b$ with $a+b\le n+1$, the point $(a,b)$ lies on at least one of the lines; and
\item exactly $k$ of the $n$ lines are sunny.
\end{itemize}

## Live Stream

Let me analyze this problem carefully.

We have:
- A line in the plane is "sunny" if it is not parallel to the x-axis, y-axis, or the line x+y=0.
- Given n ≥ 3 integer.
- Determine all nonnegative integers k such that there exist n distinct lines satisfying:
  1. For all positive integers a,b with a+b ≤ n+1, the point (a,b) lies on at least one of the lines.
  2. Exactly k of the n lines are sunny.

Let me start by analyzing the problem.

First, what does "sunny" mean? A line is sunny if it's not parallel to:
- x-axis (i.e., not horizontal)
- y-axis (i.e., not vertical)
- x+y=0 (i.e., not with slope -1)

So a line is NOT sunny (let's call it "shady") if it's parallel to one of these three directions.

The three "shady" directions are:
1. Horizontal: slope 0, equation y = c
2. Vertical: undefined slope, equation x = c
3. Slope -1: equation y = -x + c, i.e., x + y = c

Now condition 1: For all positive integers a,b with a+b ≤ n+1, the point (a,b) lies on at least one of the n lines.

The points (a,b) where a,b are positive integers and a+b ≤ n+1 form a triangular region in the grid. Let's list them:

For n=3, a+b ≤ 4, so: (1,1), (1,2), (2,1), (1,3), (2,2), (3,1)
For n=4, a+b ≤ 5: (1,1), (1,2), (2,1), (1,3), (2,2), (3,1), (1,4), (2,3), (3,2), (4,1)
In general, there are n(n+1)/2 such points.

These points form a right triangle in the first quadrant.

Now each line can cover some subset of these points. The question asks: what are the possible values of k (number of sunny lines) among the n lines, given that all these points must be covered?

Let me think about what constraints a single line can satisfy.

A line with slope m and intercept c: y = mx + c (or for vertical/horizontal lines).

For a line to cover grid points (a,b): b = ma + c.

Let me think differently. A line in the plane can contain at most 2 of the grid points (a,b) with positive integer coordinates... actually no, a line can contain infinitely many such points if it has rational slope.

Wait, but the line must cover some of the points (a,b) where a,b are positive integers and a+b ≤ n+1.

Let me think about which lines can cover at least one such point.

For a NON-sunny line:
- Horizontal (y = c): if c is a positive integer between 1 and n, it covers (1,c), (2,c), ..., (n+1-c, c).
- Vertical (x = c): if c is a positive integer between 1 and n, it covers (c,1), (c,2), ..., (c, n+1-c).
- Slope -1 (x+y = c): if c is an integer between 2 and n+1, it covers (1, c-1), (2, c-2), ..., (c-1, 1).

So a non-sunny line can cover multiple grid points. Specifically:
- Horizontal y = c covers points where a ranges from 1 to (n+1-c), so it covers (n+1-c) points.
- Vertical x = c covers points where b ranges from 1 to (n+1-c), so it covers (n+1-c) points.
- Slope -1 (x+y = c) covers points where a ranges from 1 to c-1, so it covers (c-1) points (but only those with a,b ≥ 1 and a+b ≤ n+1. If c ≤ n+1, then a+b = c ≤ n+1, so all c-1 points are valid.)

Now, for a sunny line, it can contain some of these grid points too.

Let me think about the problem more systematically.

Let's think about the "shady" lines (non-sunny lines). There are three families:
- Horizontals: y = 1, y = 2, ..., y = n (since y must be a positive integer)
- Verticals: x = 1, x = 2, ..., x = n
- Diagonals (slope -1): x+y = 2, x+y = 3, ..., x+y = n+1

Let me think about how many shady lines we need to cover all points.

Actually, let me think about this from a different angle. Consider the three directions: horizontal (H), vertical (V), and diagonal (D, slope -1).

Each point (a,b) lies on exactly:
- One horizontal: y = b
- One vertical: x = a
- One diagonal: x+y = a+b

So each point is at the intersection of exactly one H-line, one V-line, and one D-line.

If we take all horizontals y=1,...,n, that's n lines and they cover all points. 

... (content truncated to 5000 characters)

- Vertical (x = c): if c is a positive integer between 1 and n, it covers (c,1), (c,2), ..., (c, n+1-c).
- Slope -1 (x+y = c): if c is an integer between 2 and n+1, it covers (1, c-1), (2, c-2), ..., (c-1, 1).

So a non-sunny line can cover multiple grid points. Specifically:
- Horizontal y = c covers points where a ranges from 1 to (n+1-c), so it covers (n+1-c) points.
- Vertical x = c covers points where b ranges from 1 to (n+1-c), so it covers (n+1-c) points.
- Slope -1 (x+y = c) covers points where a ranges from 1 to c-1, so it covers (c-1) points (but only those with a,b ≥ 1 and a+b ≤ n+1. If c ≤ n+1, then a+b = c ≤ n+1, so all c-1 points are valid.)

Now, for a sunny line, it can contain some of these grid points too.

Let me think about the problem more systematically.

Let's think about the "shady" lines (non-sunny lines). There are three families:
- Horizontals: y = 1, y = 2, ..., y = n (since y must be a positive integer)
- Verticals: x = 1, x = 2, ..., x = n
- Diagonals (slope -1): x+y = 2, x+y = 3, ..., x+y = n+1

Let me think about how many shady lines we need to cover all points.

Actually, let me think about this from a different angle. Consider the three directions: horizontal (H), vertical (V), and diagonal (D, slope -1).

Each point (a,b) lies on exactly:
- One horizontal: y = b
- One vertical: x = a
- One diagonal: x+y = a+b

So each point is at the intersection of exactly one H-line, one V-line, and one D-line.

If we take all horizontals y=1,...,n, that's n lines and they cover all points.

    1: """
    2: EVO Agent system prompt — ported from TypeScript evo-backend/src/ai/prompt.ts.
    3: 
    4: Separated from evo_agent.py for maintainability. The prompt defines the
    5: multi-tier mandatory reasoning workflow with HALT conditions, hard rules,

    7: """
    8: 
    9: from datetime import datetime
   10: 
   11: _DATE_TOKEN = "<<CURRENT_DATE>>"
   12: 
   13: 
   14: def get_system_prompt() -> str:
   15:     """Return the EVO system prompt with the current date injected."""
   16:     date = datetime.now().strftime("%A, %B %d, %Y")
   17:     return _SYSTEM_PROMPT.replace(_DATE_TOKEN, date)
   18: 
   19: 
   20: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   21: # The full system prompt.  Uses a raw triple-quoted string so that LaTeX
   22: # backslashes (\frac, \int, \\, etc.) are literal — no Python escaping needed.
   23: # The only substitution is <<CURRENT_DATE>> replaced at runtime.
   24: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   25: 
   26: _SYSTEM_PROMPT = r"""You are EVO (Explicit-assumption Verification Orchestrator) an intelligent AI agent that performs AUTONOMOUS REASONING using a Prolog-First, derivation-based approach with explicit assumptions, proof traces, and consistency verification.
   27: 
   28: REFERENCE DATE: Today is <<CURRENT_DATE>>
   29: 
   30: ================================================================
   31: LATEX RENDERING REQUIREMENTS
   32: ================================================================
   33: 
   34: All mathematical notation must follow strict LaTeX formatting rules to prevent rendering failures:
   35: 
   36: 1. DELIMITER REQUIREMENT: Every LaTeX command MUST be wrapped in delimiters.
   37:    Valid delimiters: $...$ (inline), $$...$$ or \[...\] (display).
   38:    FORBIDDEN: bare LaTeX outside delimiters, even if it looks right as plain text.
   39: 
   40: 2. ONE EXPRESSION = ONE DELIMITER PAIR: Everything that belongs to a
   41:    single mathematical expression — the command, its arguments in braces,
   42:    its indices in brackets or parens, and any attached operators or
   43:    relations — goes inside ONE pair of delimiters.
   44:    - A command that takes arguments MUST receive them before the closing $.
   45:      WRONG: $\cmd$ {arg} or $\cmd(arg)$  — arguments outside the math region.
   46:      CORRECT: $\cmd{arg}$ or $\cmd(arg)$ — everything inside one $...$ pair.
   47:    - Do not close one $ region and open another for the same expression.
   48:      WRONG: $\int_0^{\infty}$ $\frac{\arctan(x)}{x^2+1}$ dx
   49:      CORRECT: $\int_0^{\infty} \frac{\arctan(x)}{x^2+1} dx$
   50:    - Do not put $ inside an expression that is already in math mode.
   51:      WRONG: $\left$[$\frac{u^2}{2}$$\right$]
   52:      CORRECT: $\left[\frac{u^2}{2}\right]_0^{\pi/2}$
   53: 
   54: 3. NO NESTED DELIMITERS: Never put $ or $$ inside another math expression.
   55:    CORRECT: The total is $\$100,000$ (dollar sign escaped).
   56:    WRONG: The total is $$100,000$ (nested delimiters).
   57: 
   58: 4. ALIGNED / CASES: Multi-line environments stay in ONE display-math block.
   59:    CORRECT:
   60:      $$\begin{aligned} a &= b \\ c &= d \end{aligned}$$
   61:      $$a(i) = \begin{cases} 0 & i=0 \\ 1 & i=1 \\ 2(i-1)! & i\ge 2 \end{cases}$$
   62:    WRONG: splitting the equals sign or individual cases into separate blocks.
   63:    Each new row uses \\ (two backslashes) followed by & for the alignment point.
   64: 
   65: 
   66: ================================================================
   67: CORE PRINCIPLE — EVIDENCE-BASED REASONING ACROSS THREE TIERS
   68: ================================================================
   69: 
   70: A task is SOLVED only when its evidence requirements are met.
   71: The evidence type depends on the task tier (determined at TRIAGE):
   72: 
   73:   MATHS:    The single tier for mathematical work: computation, derivation,
   74:             construction, classification, counterexample search, case
   75:             analysis, informal proof, or formal proof.  Gauge the required
   76:             rigor from the request: computational, derivational, proof, or
   77:             formal.  Evidence requirements by rigor:
   78:               computational — python_exec/sympy_exec computation with
   79:                 computation_check; maths_problem tracks the result.
   80:               derivational — symbolic derivation or transformation with
   81:                 maths_problem stage=derive + verify_final.
   82:               proof — construction/existence and/or exclusion/impossibility
   83:                 evidence with maths_problem stage=derive + verify_final.
   84:               formal — Lean 4 verification via prove_problem (frontier
   85:                 decomposition + parallel workers) as a subordinate proof
   86:                 workflow, then maths_problem verify_final as the final
   87:                 MATHS gate.  An ordinary proof may fall back to MATHS
   88:                 proof verification if Lean is infeasible.
   89:   CODE:     Source files, repository metadata, build/test output, and
   90:             documentation are the evidence.  CODE inherits REASON's logical
   91:             rigor.  Use Prolog to encode code structure facts, map
   92:             dependencies, and derive conclusions about impact, completeness,
   93:             and correctness. Code evidence comes first; for complex tasks,
   94:             Prolog reasoning may derive conclusions over that evidence. Use an
   95:             explicit reasoning ledger for observations, hypotheses, support,
   96:             contradictions, and verification results.  Code structure maps
   97:             naturally to Prolog facts/rules, so Prolog should be used when
   98:             source evidence can be profitably formalized as predicates after
   99:             the relevant code has been inspected.
  100:   REASON:   Prolog derives conclusions from facts and rules, with proof

    1: # CLAUDE.md
    2: 
    3: This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.
    4: 
    5: ## Project overview
    6: 
    7: EVO Web is a standalone FastAPI web UI for the Python EVO Agent — an AI agent that uses Prolog-first, derivation-based reasoning with explicit assumptions and formal verification. It is deployed as a Hugging Face Docker Space.
    8: 
    9: ## Commands
   10: 
   11: ```bash
   12: # Local run (Windows PowerShell)
   13: .\run.ps1
   14: # Open http://127.0.0.1:7861
   15: 
   16: # Local run (Windows CMD)
   17: .\run.bat
   18: 
   19: # Docker build and run
   20: docker build -t evo-web .
   21: docker run -p 7860:7860 --env-file .env evo-web
   22: 
   23: # Direct uvicorn (after pip install -r requirements-web.txt)
   24: set PYTHONPATH=.
   25: python -m uvicorn run_evo_web_login:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 7861
   26: ```
   27: 
   28: Tests exist but have no runner/CI; they use `unittest` and can be invoked individually:
   29: 
   30: ```bash
   31: python -m unittest test_event_buffer.py
   32: python -m unittest test_evo_gates.py
   33: ```
   34: 
   35: ## Architecture
   36: 
   37: ```
   38: run_evo_web_login.py   FastAPI app: auth, conversation CRUD, SSE streaming, HTML views
   39: run_manager.py         RunManager: worker threads, event channels, replay buffers
   40: evo_agent.py           Core agent: think() pipeline, tool-calling loop, 3-tier triage
   41: evo_context.py         Context window manager: offload, archive, LLM-driven compression
   42: evo_prompt.py          Full system prompt (injected with current date at runtime)
   43: evo_cot_monitor.py     CoT monitor: third-person observer, running summary, self-observations
   44: evo_evidence.py        Progressive evidence ledger for deterministic groundedness checking
   45: evo_gate_breach_ledger.py  Mid-loop gate-policy breach detection and correction
   46: config.py              Env-based config; provider-aware token/throttling params
   47: model_registry.py      Model name helpers for multi-model routing
   48: apply_code_changes.py  Utility to patch CODE tier changes into agent/prompt files
   49: ```
   50: 
   51: The agent uses a **3-tier triage** that determines which tools and reasoning strategy to apply:
   52: - **REASON** — logical/philosophical reasoning (Prolog derivation primary). All requests default here unless classified as MATHS or CODE.
   53: - **MATHS** — mathematical work: computation, derivation, proof, classification. Uses `math_rigor` to select mode: `computational` (Python/SymPy primary), `derivational`, `proof`, or `formal` (Lean 4 verification primary).
   54: - **CODE** — code/config/repository work (source evidence primary)
   55: 
   56: MATHS/formal is NOT a separate tier — it is a rigor sub-mode within MATHS, triggered by `math_rigor: "formal"`. MATHS/formal tasks use `prove_problem` and `solve_lean_eval_problem` as their primary orchestrators, with Lean 4 as the verification authority. The four `math_rigor` values are: `computational`, `derivational`, `proof`, `formal`.
   57: 
   58: Each tier has its own scratch pad orchestrator and HALT conditions defined in the system prompt.
   59: 
   60: ### Gate system and groundedness
   61: 
   62: EVO applies a **three-layer gating system** to enforce reasoning quality:
   63: 
   64: 1. **Layer 1 (per-tool):** Runtime checks — `_check_workflow_gate()` enforces the required tool-call sequence per tier, and `_check_tool_authorization()` enforces capability flags (`_TOOL_CAPABILITY_MAP`), deduplication, and tier restrictions (e.g., MATHS blocks `web_search`/`web_browse`/`github`).
   65: 2. **Layer 1.5 (mid-loop):** `GateBreachLedger` (`evo_gate_breach_ledger.py`) detects gate-policy breaches during tool execution — inspects tool results for quality issues (e.g., `sorry` in Lean proofs, missing Prolog on complex REASON tasks), inspects assistant messages for pattern breaches (missing TRIAGE declarations, bare LaTeX, DSML leakage), and records workflow-step gaps. Can inject corrective feedback immediately. 25 gate types exist across three detection points: `tool_result`, `assistant_message`, `workflow_state`.
   66: 3. **Layer 2 (answer-time):** `_enforce_lean_policy()` runs 25 gates on the final response text before it reaches the user. A **gate retry loop** detects fixable failures and re-enters the tool loop with injected feedback before giving up (max 2 correction attempts per breach).
   67: 
   68: After gating, the **groundedness audit** (`evo_evidence.py`) checks that response claims are traceable to tool-produced evidence. The `EvidenceLedger` extracts structured claims from tool results as they execute (claim types: `derivation_count`, `conclusion_observation_grounded`, `conclusion_derived`, `lean_verified`, `lean_theorem_verified`, `prove_verified`, `sha256_match`, `computation_verified`, `maths_verified`, etc.), then cross-references response claims deterministically — no LLM needed for Prolog-structured evidence. Claims that can't be matched get an LLM fallback check. Ungrounded claims trigger a "revise or label" feedback loop.
   69: 
   70: ### CoT Monitor
   71: 
   72: `evo_cot_monitor.py` provides a `CotMonitor` class that acts as a third-person observer of the primary agent's chain-of-thought. It:
   73: - Maintains a **running summary** of the entire CoT trajectory across all turns
   74: - Every 3 tool-loop iterations, feeds the latest CoT segment (asynchronously) to a separate LLM
   75: - The LLM produces a **self-observation** — a one-paragraph reflection on patterns, omissions, confidence shifts, or framing choices visible in the CoT
   76: - Observations are injected as synthetic user messages before the primary's next LLM call, and forwarded to the web UI trace log as `[Monitor]` events
   77: - Disabled by setting `MONITOR_ENABLED=false`; uses its own model via `MONITOR_MODEL` (defaults to same as primary)
   78: 
   79: ### EvoSubAgent (worker agents)
   80: 
   81: `tools/evo_subagent.py` provides persistent worker `EvoAgent` instances with private inbox/outbox queues. Workers can only talk to the Boss (primary EvoAgent) — no access to the SSE stream, main conversation history, or user-facing output. Workers share a `ProofInsightKB` for proof specialist results across workers. The `SubAgentRegistry` in `evo_agent.py` manages worker lifecycle.
   82: 
   83: ### Key subpackages
   84: 
   85: | Package | Role |
   86: |---|---|
   87: | `mind/` | Alternate AI agent runtime (`mind/core.py:Mind`); EVO can spawn Mind subagents. Shared substrate (`mind/substrate.py`) provides tool-call parsing, iteration, and retry helpers used by both EVO and Mind. Rate limiter (`mind/rate_limiter.py`) provides `chat_completion_with_retry`. |
   88: | `reasoning/` | Prolog-based reasoner (`reasoner.py`), proof checker (`proof_checker.py`), proof IR (`proof_ir.py`), math IR (`math_ir.py`), expression IR (`expression_ir.py`), policy engine (`policy.py`) |
   89: | `tools/` | Tool implementations — see Tool catalog below |
   90: | `knowledge/` | Knowledge base (`base.py`), artifact registry (`artifacts.py`), focus scoring (`focus.py`) |
   91: | `lean/` | Lean 4 integration: mathlib catalog and executor (`mathlib.py`), proof assembler (`proof_assembler.py`), structure parser (`structure_parser.py`) |
   92: | `extraction/` | Code artifact extraction (`code_artifacts.py`, `github_code_artifacts.py`), fact extraction (`facts.py`), Prolog assertion IR (`ir.py`), semantic model validation (`model_validator.py`) |
   93: | `prolog/` | Prolog knowledge base (`base.pl`) and session-specific KB files |
   94: | `docs/` | Design docs: `gates_and_groundedness.md`, `tier_workflows.md` |
   95: 
   96: ### Tool catalog
   97: 
   98: Tools defined in `EVO_TOOLS` (evo_agent.py). Implementation classes exported from `tools/__init__.py`.
   99: 
  100: | Tool name | File | Tier / Capability | Purpose |
  101: |---|---|---|---|
  102: | `prolog_exec` | (via `PrologReasoner`) | All tiers except MATHS/formal | Execute Prolog code; stateful accumulated mode for REASON/MATHS/CODE tiers |
  103: | `python_exec` | `tools/python_executor.py` | `computation_programmatic` | Execute Python code (numpy, scipy, sklearn, sympy available) |
  104: | `sympy_exec` | (via `PythonExecutor`) | `computation_programmatic` | Execute SymPy code (same executor as python_exec; separate tool name for model clarity) |
  105: | `lean4_exec` | (via `LeanMathlib`) | `formal_verification` | Execute Lean 4 code and verify theorems (NO sorries; requires `lean4_exit_code(0)` + `status: lean4_verified`) |
  106: | `lean4_probe` | (via `LeanMathlib`) | `formal_verification` | Probe Lean 4 code with sorries allowed; incremental proof building |
  107: | `mathlib_check` | (via `LeanMathlib`) | `formal_verification` | Check a single mathlib lemma |
  108: | `mathlib_search` | (via `LeanMathlib`) | `formal_verification` | Search mathlib for lemmas by natural-language query |
  109: | `batch_mathlib_check` | (via `LeanMathlib`) | `formal_verification` | Batch-check multiple mathlib lemma names |
  110: | `prove_problem` | `tools/proof_solver.py` | `formal_verification` | MATHS/formal stage controller (frontier-lemma decomposition + parallel worker workflow). Stages: `start` → `statement_skeleton` → `frontier_plan` → `register_frontier_lemma` → `verify_frontier_lemma` → `block_frontier_lemma` → `prove_ready` → `verify_final` |
  111: | `maths_problem` | `tools/maths_solver.py` | `mathematical_reasoning` | MATHS-tier stage controller for derivation/proof/classification. Stages: `start` → `model` → `explore` → `derive` → `verify_step` → `verify_final` |
  112: | `lean_eval_submission_check` | `tools/lean_eval_submission.py` | `formal_verification` | Check a Lean-Eval submission (lake build + sorry/admit scan) |
  113: | `lean_eval_problem` | `tools/lean_eval_problem.py` | `formal_verification` | Lean-Eval problem workflow (list unsolved, inspect, prepare workspace, read/write files, grep, tail) |
  114: | `solve_lean_eval_problem` | `tools/lean_eval_solver.py` | `formal_verification` | Full Lean-Eval solve/fix orchestrator. Stages: `start` → `solution_ready` → `write_verified` → `preflight`/`ci_verify` → `logs`/`state`. Final authority is GitHub Actions CI, not local `lean4_exec`. Includes `save_attempt` for checkpointing partial progress. |
  115: | `solve_matharena_problem` | `tools/matharena_solver.py` | `mathematical_reasoning` | MathArena solve orchestrator |
  116: | `web_search` | `tools/web_search.py` | `web_lookup` | Web search (via LangSearch API) |
  117: | `web_browse` | `tools/web_browse.py` | `web_lookup` | Fetch and parse web pages (Playwright) — optional CSS selector for targeted extraction |
  118: | `github` | `tools/github_public.py` | `web_lookup` | GitHub API: read files/dirs, query REST endpoints, profile-scoped writes |
  119: | `git` | `tools/git_executor.py` | `computation_programmatic` | Local git operations in the working directory (clone, log, diff, grep, map) |
  120: | `code_scratch_pad` | `tools/code_scratch_pad.py` | `web_lookup` | CODE-tier persistent scratch pad (test1-deepthought/evo_code_scratch_pad). Modes: inspect/plan/implement/verify/diagnose/retrospective |

  546: The brute force approach is too slow. Let me think more carefully and write a more efficient algorithm.
  547: 
  548: The problem is about covering the triangular grid points with n lines, where exactly k are sunny (not parallel to x-axis, y-axis, or x+y=0).
  549: 
  550: Key structural insight: Each point (a,b) with positive integers a,b and a+b ≤ n+1 lies at the intersection of exactly:
  551: - One horizontal line: y = b
  552: - One vertical line: x = a
  553: - One diagonal line: x + y = a + b
  554: 
  555: This forms a 3-uniform hypergraph on the triangle.
  556: 
  557: Now, I need to think about which subsets of points can lie on a single sunny line. For a sunny line (m ≠ 0, -1, ∞), it can contain at most one point from each row, each column, and each "anti-diagonal" (x+y = constant). 
  558: 
  559: For points (a_i, b_i) on a line y = mx + c with slope m = p/q (in lowest terms), the points must satisfy b_i = (p/q)a_i + c, so they're of the form (a, (p/q)a + c). If the line passes through integer grid points, then when a increases by q, b increases by p.
  560: 
  561: So the set of points on a sunny line forms an arithmetic progression in both coordinates.

  587: Actually, let me think about the key constraint. For a sunny line to pass through k points from the triangle, those k points must have distinct x-coordinates, distinct y-coordinates, and distinct x+y sums. And they must be collinear.

  589: For n lines to cover all n(n+1)/2 points, and with each line covering at most... well, how many points can a sunny line cover?
  590: 
  591: A sunny line with slope p/q (reduced) and the right intercept could cover several points. For instance, y = (1/2)x + c: when x increases by 2, y increases by 1.
  592: 
  593: But actually, the maximum number of points a single line can cover from the triangle {(a,b): a,b ≥ 1, a+b ≤ n+1} is n (the length of the longest side). A horizontal line y = c covers n+1-c points, max n. A vertical line x = c covers n+1-c points, max n. A diagonal x+y = c covers c-1 points, max n. And a sunny line can cover at most n points too (since it has at most one point per row).
  594: 
  595: So each line covers at most n points, and there are n lines covering n(n+1)/2 points. The average is (n+1)/2.

  601: Let me try to prove the general result.
  602: 
  603: Consider the three families of "shady" lines: horizontals H_i (y=i), verticals V_j (x=j), and diagonals D_k (x+y=k).
  604: 
  605: Given a set of lines that covers all points, we can think about the "shady" lines that are included and which points they cover.
  606: 
  607: Key Lemma: If a point (a,b) is not covered by any horizontal line y = b, any vertical line x = a, or any diagonal line x+y = a+b in our set, then it must be covered by a sunny line.
  608: 
  609: But more importantly, each point (a,b) has three "natural" shady lines through it. If we don't include any of these three, we need a sunny line through (a,b).

  615: Wait: if two points have different x, different y, and different x+y, does that guarantee the slope is not 0, -1, or ∞?
  616: - Slope = (y2-y1)/(x2-x1). If x1 ≠ x2, slope ≠ ∞. ✓
  617: - If y1 ≠ y2, slope ≠ 0. ✓  
  618: - If (x1+y1) ≠ (x2+y2), then (y2-y1)/(x2-x1) ≠ -1. Let's check: slope = -1 means y2-y1 = -(x2-x1), so y2-y1 = -x2+x1, so x1+y1 = x2+y2. So if sums differ, slope ≠ -1. ✓
  619: 
  620: So two points can be on a sunny line iff they have different x, different y, and different sum.
  621: 
  622: For three points to be on the same sunny line, they additionally need to be collinear.
  623: 
  624: Now, let's think about which sets of points can be paired into sunny-line-compatible pairs.
  625: 
  626: Actually, let me think about this from the perspective of "matching" or "graph coloring".
  627: 
  628: Consider the set of points that are NOT covered by any shady line in our configuration. These must be covered by sunny lines. Each sunny line can cover at most... well, it depends.
  629: 
  630: But here's another approach. Instead of trying to find arbitrary configurations, let me think about what happens if we use exactly h horizontal lines, v vertical lines, d diagonal lines, and s = n-h-v-d sunny lines.
  631: 
  632: For each point (a,b), let's track which of the three corresponding shady lines (y=b, x=a, x+y=a+b) we've included.
  633: 
  634: If we include all three for every point, we've covered everything with n=3n... wait, that doesn't make sense since we're limited to n lines.
  635: 
  636: OK let me think about a different structuring. The triangle has n(n+1)/2 points, and we have n lines. Each line can cover many points.
  637: 
  638: Let me think about the maximum possible number of sunny lines.
  639: 
  640: For k = n, all n lines are sunny. Can we cover all points? For n=3, yes (I found a construction). For n=4?
  641:

  643: 
  644: Points for n=4:
  645: (1,1) (1,2) (1,3) (1,4)
  646: (2,1) (2,2) (2,3)
  647: (3,1) (3,2)
  648: (4,1)

  650: I need to partition these into 4 sets, each lying on a sunny line.
  651: 
  652: Let me try finding 4 sunny lines covering all points.
  653: 
  654: For a sunny line to cover multiple points, they must have distinct x,y,sum and be collinear.
  655: 
  656: Let me try:
  657: Line 1: (1,1), (2,2), (3,3) — but (3,3) has sum 6 > 5, so not in the set for n=4.
  658: Line 1: (1,1), (2,2) — slope 1, sunny. Pts covered: {(1,1),(2,2)}.
  659: 
  660: Line 2: (1,2), (3,1) — slope (1-2)/(3-1) = -1/2, sunny. Pts covered: {(1,2),(3,1)}.
  661: 
  662: Line 3: (1,3), (2,1) — slope (1-3)/(2-1) = -2, sunny. Pts covered: {(1,3),(2,1)}.
  663: 
  664: Line 4: (1,4), (2,3), (3,2), (4,1) — wait, let me check these:
  665: (1,4) and (2,3): slope = (3-4)/(2-1) = -1, diagonal, shady! 
  666: So (1,4) and (2,3) cannot be on a sunny line together.
  667: 
  668: Hmm. (1,4) has sum 5. (4,1) has sum 5. They're on the same diagonal x+y=5 (which is the hypotenuse for n=4).
  669: 
  670: So the hypotenuse points all have sum n+1 = 5 and all lie on the diagonal D_{n+1}. They can't be paired together on a sunny line.
  671: 
  672: Let me try:
  673: Line 4: (1,4), (3,2) — slope (2-4)/(3-1) = -2/2 = -1, diagonal, shady! No.
  674: 
  675: Line 4: (1,4), (2,3) — slope -1, shady.
  676: Line 4: (1,4), (3,1) — but (3,1) is already used.
  677: Line 4: (1,4), (4,1) — diagonal, shady.
  678: 
  679: Hmm, (1,4) can only be on a sunny line with points that don't have x=1 (which would be vertical), y=4 (which would be horizontal), or sum 5 (which would be diagonal).
  680: 
  681: Points remaining after lines 1-3: (1,4), (2,3), (3,2), (4,1). These all have sum 5! So any two of them share sum 5 and cannot be on a sunny line together.
  682: 
  683: Can a single sunny line cover only one of these points? Yes, e.g., line through (1,4) with slope 1: y = x + 3, passes through (1,4). Does it pass through any other point in the triangle? x=2: y=5, but 2+5=7>5, not in triangle. x=3: y=6, 3+6=9>5, not in triangle. So it covers only (1,4).
  684: 
  685: Similarly, a line through (2,3) with any sunny slope covers only (2,3).
  686: 
  687: So 4 sunny lines can cover at most: 2+2+2+1 = 7 points (using the 3 pairings from n=3 plus 4 singletons). But we have 10 points to cover.

  689: Actually wait. With 4 sunny lines, if each covers 2 points, that's 8 points. We need 10. So some lines must cover 3+ points.

  691: A sunny line covering 3 points: these 3 points must have distinct x, y, sum and be collinear. For n=4, can I find such a line?
  692: 
  693: For collinear points in arithmetic progression: (a, b), (a+q, b+p), (a+2q, b+2p) with slope p/q.
  694: 
  695: Points (1,1), (2,3), (3,5): slope (3-1)/(2-1)=2, so line y=2x-1. Check: x=1,y=1; x=2,y=3; x=3,y=5. But (3,5) has sum 8 > 5, not in triangle.
  696: Points (1,1), (3,2), (5,3): slope (2-1)/(3-1)=1/2. (5,3) has sum 8 > 5. Not in triangle.
  697: 
  698: Points (1,2), (2,4), (3,6): slope 2. (2,4) sum 6 > 5. Not in triangle.
  699: 
  700: Hmm, for n=4, the triangle is small. Let me see if any 3 points are collinear and can all lie in the triangle.
  701: 
  702: Points: (1,1), (2,2), (3,3) — collinear (slope 1) but (3,3) sum = 6 > 5, so not in triangle for n=4.
  703: (1,1), (3,2) — these two: slope 1/2. Add (5,3) — not in triangle.
  704: (1,2), (2,3), (3,4) — slope 1, but (3,4) sum = 7 > 5.
  705: 
  706: So for n=4, I don't think any sunny line can contain 3 points from the triangle.
  707: 
  708: Maximum points per sunny line for n=4: 2.
  709: 
  710: With 4 sunny lines each covering at most 2 points: at most 8 points covered. But we need 10. So k=4 is impossible for n=4!
  711: 
  712: This is a key insight. The maximum number of points a sunny line can cover determines the maximum k.
  713: 
  714: For n, what's the maximum number of points on a sunny line within the triangle {(a,b): a,b ≥ 1, a+b ≤ n+1}?
  715: 
  716: A sunny line has slope p/q in lowest terms (q > 0, p ≠ 0, -q). The points on it form an arithmetic progression: (a, b), (a+q, b+p), (a+2q, b+2p), ... with the constraint that both coordinates are ≥ 1 and their sum ≤ n+1.
  717: 
  718: The maximum number of such points is roughly n/max(|p|,|q|) in at least one dimension... but we can choose p/q smartly.
  719: 
  720: For slope 1 (p=q=1): points (1,1), (2,2), (3,3), ..., with constraint 2a ≤ n+1, so a ≤ (n+1)/2. So at most floor((n+1)/2) points.
  721: 
  722: For slope 2 (p=2,q=1): points (1,2), (2,4), (3,6), ..., constraint: a + (2a) = 3a ≤ n+1, so a ≤ (n+1)/3. At most floor((n+1)/3).
  723: 
  724: For slope -2 (p=-2,q=1): points (a, -2a+c) — solving b = -2a + c. If through (1,n): c = n+2. Points: a=1: b=n. a=2: b=n-2. a=3: b=n-4. Constraint: a+b = 3a-2? Actually a+b = a + (-2a+n+2) = n+2-a ≤ n+1, so a ≥ 1. And b ≥ 1: -2a+n+2 ≥ 1, so a ≤ (n+1)/2.
  725: 
  726: Hmm, this gives about n/2 points for slope -2.
  727: 
  728: For slope 1/2 (p=1,q=2): points (1,?), (3,?), (5,?)... let me use the line through (1,1): y = x/2 + 1/2. Check: (1,1): 1=0.5+0.5 ✓. (3,2): 2=1.5+0.5 ✓. (5,3): 5+3=8>n+1 for n=4. For general n, constraint: a + (a/2+1/2) ≤ n+1, so 3a/2 ≤ n+1/2, so 3a ≤ 2n+1, so a ≤ (2n+1)/3. At most about 2n/3 / 2 = n/3... hmm.
  729: 
  730: Actually let me think about this more carefully. For slope p/q with p,q > 0 and p,q reasonably small, the maximum number of points is limited by:
  731: - From a+b constraint: a + b = a + (p/q)a + c = (p+q)a/q + c ≤ n+1
  732: - From b ≥ 1: (p/q)a + c ≥ 1
  733: 
  734: The maximum size is roughly n * q / (p+q), give or take.
  735: 
  736: To maximize the number of points, we want p+q as small as possible while p/q ≠ 0, -1, ∞. So p=1, q=1 (slope 1) gives p+q=2, with roughly n/2 points.
  737: 
  738: But wait, we also need p/q ≠ 0, so p ≠ 0. And p/q ≠ -1, so p ≠ -q. And slope ≠ ∞, so q ≠ 0.
  739: 
  740: The minimum possible value of |p|+q subject to p/q ≠ 0, -1, ∞ and p/q is reduced is... well, p=±1, q=1 gives |p|+q=2. Constraints: ±1/1 ≠ 0, -1, ∞. p/q = 1 (slope 1): allowed! p/q = -1: not allowed. So p=1, q=1 (slope 1) is the best.
  741: 
  742: For slope 1, the line y = x + c. Points on this line in the triangle: (a, a+c) where a ≥ 1, a+c ≥ 1, and 2a+c ≤ n+1. For c = 0: (1,1), (2,2), ..., (floor((n+1)/2), floor((n+1)/2)). That's floor((n+1)/2) points.
  743: 
  744: For slope 1, c = 1: (1,2), (2,3), ..., (floor(n/2), floor(n/2)+1). That's floor(n/2) points.
  745: 
  746: So a sunny line with slope 1 covers at most floor((n+1)/2) points.
  747: 
  748: For slope 2 (p=2, q=1): y = 2x + c. Points: (1,2+c), (2,4+c), ... with constraint 3a + c ≤ n+1. For c = -1: (2,3), (3,5), ... For n=5: (2,3) only (since 3a-1 ≤ 6, a ≤ 7/3, a≤2). So at most about n/3 points.
  749: 
  750: For slope -2 (p=-2, q=1): y = -2x + c. Points: (a, -2a+c). Constraint: a + (-2a+c) = c-a ≤ n+1, so a ≥ c-n-1. And b = -2a+c ≥ 1, so a ≤ (c-1)/2. For c = n+1 (going through (1,n-1)): a ranges satisfying max(1, (n+1)-(n+1)) = 1 ≤ a ≤ n/2. So about n/2 points.
  751: 
  752: For slope 1/2 (p=1, q=2): y = x/2 + c. Points: (2k+1, k+c) or (2k, k+c) depending on c. For c=1/2: (1,1), (3,2), (5,3), ... Constraint: a + a/2 + 1/2 ≤ n+1, so 3a/2 ≤ n+1/2, so a ≤ (2n+1)/3. Number of such a with same parity as the first: roughly n/3.
  753: 
  754: So slope 1 gives the most points: about n/2.
  755: 
  756: For n=3, max is floor((3+1)/2) = 2. Three sunny lines each covering 2 points → 6 points covered. Works!
  757: 
  758: For n=4, max is floor((4+1)/2) = 2. Four sunny lines each covering at most 2 → at most 8. But we have 10 points. Can't work!
  759: 
  760: So k=4 is impossible for n=4.
  761: 
  762: What about k=3 for n=4? Three sunny lines cover at most 6 points. One shady line can cover at most n=4 points. So at most 10 points. That's tight.

  948:

  950: For k=2: 2 × 3 + 3 × 5 = 21 > 15.
  951: For k=1: 1 × 3 + 4 × 5 = 23 > 15.
  952: For k=0: 5 × 5 = 25 > 15.

  956: Let me explore more systematically but efficiently with a smarter algorithm.
  957: 
  958: Actually, let me try to think about the problem more theoretically.
  959: 
  960: The key observation: The points (a,b) with a,b positive integers and a+b ≤ n+1 form a triangular lattice. The three "shady" directions are the three natural directions of this lattice.
  961: 
  962: Consider the set of lines we choose. Each point must be covered by at least one line.
  963: 
  964: Let me think about an information-theoretic or combinatorial argument. Each sunny line can intersect the triangle in at most n points (one per row), but actually the maximum is floor(n/2)+1 for slope 1, roughly.
  965: 
  966: Actually wait, I computed the maximum for slope 1 is floor((n+1)/2). Let me verify this is actually the maximum possible.
  967: 
  968: For a line y = mx + c with m ≠ 0, -1, ∞. Points on it in the triangle: (a, ma+c) where a ≥ 1, ma+c ≥ 1, and a+ma+c = (m+1)a + c ≤ n+1.
  969: 
  970: If m > 0, then a ranges from max(1, ceil((1-c)/m)) to floor((n+1-c)/(m+1)). The maximum number of integer a values depends on c and m.
  971: 
  972: For m = 1 (slope 1): a from max(1, ceil(1-c)) to floor((n+1-c)/2). To maximize this range, set c = 1-n... hmm, that gives a from 1 to floor((2n)/(2)) = n? No wait: floor((n+1-(1-n))/2) = floor((2n)/2) = n. But b = a + (1-n), so b starts at 2-n, which is negative for n>2. So b ≥ 1 constraint gives a ≥ n.
  973: 
  974: Actually the constraint b = a + c ≥ 1 gives a ≥ 1-c. For c = 0, a ≥ 1, and a ≤ floor((n+1)/2). So points (1,1),(2,2),...,(floor((n+1)/2), floor((n+1)/2)). That's floor((n+1)/2) points.
  975: 
  976: For m > 1 (steeper), the range shrinks because (m+1) in the denominator is larger.
  977: 
  978: For -1 < m < 0, say m = -1/2: a ranges from... b = -a/2 + c ≥ 1, so a ≤ 2(c-1). And a+b = a/2 + c ≤ n+1, so a ≤ 2(n+1-c). Combined: a ≤ min(2(c-1), 2(n+1-c)). Maximized when c-1 ≈ n+1-c, i.e., c ≈ (n+2)/2. Then a ≤ n+1. But also a starts at 1. Number of a values: roughly n. But wait, does the line pass through integer points for all these a values? Since m = -1/2, going from a to a+2 increases b by 1. So integer points occur every 2 steps in a. So at most about n/2 points.
  979: 
  980: For m < -1, similar analysis.
  981: 
  982: So the maximum number of lattice points from the triangle on a sunny line is floor((n+1)/2), achieved by slope 1 (m=1) with c=0: points (1,1),(2,2),...,(floor((n+1)/2), floor((n+1)/2)).
  983: 
  984: Now, this gives us the maximum. Let me denote M(n) = floor((n+1)/2) as the maximum number of points per sunny line.
  985: 
  986: For n=3: M=2. 3 sunny lines × 2 = 6 = total points. Works.
  987: For n=4: M=2. 4 sunny × 2 = 8 < 10. So k=4 impossible.
  988: For n=5: M=3. 5 sunny × 3 = 15 = total points. Potentially works.
  989: For n=6: M=3. 6 sunny × 3 = 18. Total points = 21. So k=6 impossible.
  990: For n=7: M=4. 7 sunny × 4 = 28. Total = 28. Potentially works.
  991: 
  992: So the obvious pattern: k=n is possible when n(n+1)/2 ≤ n * floor((n+1)/2), i.e., (n+1)/2 ≤ floor((n+1)/2). This holds when n+1 is even, i.e., n is odd.
  993: 
  994: So k=n is possible for odd n, impossible for even n.
  995: 
  996: Now, for the general problem, I need to determine ALL k.
  997: 
  998: Let me think about this in terms of how many points the shady lines cover and how many the sunny lines must cover.
  999: 
 1000: If we have s sunny lines, they cover at most s*M(n) = s*floor((n+1)/2) points from the triangle. The remaining n(n+1)/2 - s*floor((n+1)/2) points must be covered by the n-s shady lines.
 1001: 
 1002: A shady line of type H_c covers n+1-c points. A shady line of type V_c covers n+1-c points. A shady line of type D_c covers c-1 points.
 1003: 
 1004: So the maximum coverage by h horizontals, v verticals, and d diagonals is:
 1005: - H_c for c=1,...,h: covers n+1-1 + n+1-2 + ... + n+1-h = hn + h - h(h+1)/2 = h(n - (h-1)/2)
 1006: Wait, let me compute: sum_{c=1}^h (n+1-c) = h(n+1) - h(h+1)/2.
 1007: 
 1008: Similarly for v verticals (choosing the best ones, c=1,...,v): v(n+1) - v(v+1)/2.
 1009: 
 1010: For d diagonals (choosing c = n+1, n, ..., n+2-d): sum_{c=n+2-d}^{n+1} (c-1) = d(n+1) - d(d+1)/2.
 1011: 
 1012: So total coverage by h+v+d shady lines is at most:
 1013: (h+v+d)(n+1) - (h(h+1) + v(v+1) + d(d+1))/2

 1429: Wait, let me think about this in terms of "slope classes." Every non-vertical line has a slope m. If m is not 0 or -1, it's sunny. The key property is:
 1430: 
 1431: A sunny line with slope m can contain points (a,b) where b - ma is constant. This means the values b - ma are the same for all points on the line.
 1432: 
 1433: For slope 1: b - a is constant. So points with the same b-a are on the same slope-1 line.
 1434: For slope -1/2: b + a/2 is constant. So 2b + a is constant.
 1435: For slope -2: b + 2a is constant.
 1436: 
 1437: Each of these "invariants" partitions the points. Points on the same slope-m line have the same value of b-ma.
 1438: 
 1439: The three shady directions are:
 1440: - Horizontal (m=0): b is constant
 1441: - Vertical (m=∞): a is constant
 1442: - Diagonal (m=-1): b+a is constant
 1443: 
 1444: A sunny line (m ≠ 0, -1, ∞) has the invariant I = b - ma which must be constant.
 1445: 
 1446: Now, for the problem: we select n lines. For each point, at least one of the three shady lines through it must be selected, OR it must lie on a selected sunny line.

 1453:

 3323: - k = 2: Impossible for n ≥ 3 (structural reason: any 2 sunny lines can cover at most 2+2=4 interior points, but any n-2 shady lines leave at least 3 points with pairwise coordinate conflicts)
 3324: - k = 3: Possible for n = 3,4,5,6; impossible for n ≥ 7 (by counting argument)
 3325: - k = n: Possible only for n = 3; impossible for n ≥ 4 (by counting: n·M(n) < n(n+1)/2 for n ≥ 4)

 3404: Suppose we have a configuration with 2 sunny lines. Let the 2 sunny lines be L1 and L2. All other n-2 lines are shady (horizontals, verticals, or diagonals).

 3406: Consider the points (1,1), (1,n), (n,1). These three points are at the "corners" of the triangle. Their corresponding shady lines are:
 3407: - (1,1): H1 (y=1), V1 (x=1), D2 (x+y=2)
 3408: - (1,n): V1 (x=1), D_{n+1} (x+y=n+1)
 3409: - (n,1): H1 (y=1), D_{n+1} (x+y=n+1)

 3411: If we choose at most 2 of {H1, V1, D_{n+1}} as shady lines, then at least one of the three corners is not covered by its corresponding shady lines. That corner would need to be covered by a sunny line.

 3413: Since there are 3 corners and only 2 sunny lines, the two sunny lines can cover at most 2 of the 3 corners directly.

But we only have 2 sunny lines, and each can cover at most one corner (since all three corners have either same x (1,1) and (1,n) or same y (1,1) and (n,1) or same sum (1,n) and (n,1)).  3426: (1,1) and (1,n): same x → vertical, can't.

 4395: /-- A line in the plane is represented by coefficients (a,b,c) for equation ax+by=c. -/

 4397:   (a : ℚ) (b : ℚ) (c : ℚ)
 4398:   (nonzero : a ≠ 0 ∨ b ≠ 0)
 4399: 
 4400: /-- A line is "sunny" if it is not parallel to the x-axis, the y-axis, or the line x+y=0. -/
 4401: def isSunny (L : Line) : Prop :=
 4402:   L.a ≠ 0 ∧ L.b ≠ 0 ∧ L.a ≠ L.b
 4403: 
 4404: /-- The triangular set of points (a,b) with a,b ≥ 1 and a+b ≤ n+1. -/
 4405: def T (n : ℕ) : Set (ℕ × ℕ) :=
 4406:   {p | p.1 ≥ 1 ∧ p.2 ≥ 1 ∧ p.1 + p.2 ≤ n+1}
 4407: 
 4408: /-- A point (a,b) lies on a line ax+by=c. -/
 4409: def liesOn (p : ℕ × ℕ) (L : Line) : Prop :=
 4410:   L.a * (p.1 : ℚ) + L.b * (p.2 : ℚ) = L.c
 4411: 
 4412: /-- A set of lines covers T_n if every point of T_n lies on at least one line. -/
 4413: def covers (lines : Set Line) (n : ℕ) : Prop :=
 4414:   ∀ p, p ∈ T n → ∃ L ∈ lines, liesOn p L
 4415: 
 4416: /-- Main theorem: For integer n ≥ 3, determine all nonnegative integers k
 4417:     such that there exist n distinct lines covering T_n with exactly k sunny lines. -/

%% --- Harness ---
:- dynamic active_assumption/1.
prove(Goal, proved(Goal)) :- call(Goal).
contradictory_pair(_X, _Y) :- false.
inconsistent :- contradictory_pair(_, _).

%% --- Source facts from codebase inspection ---
%% Core agent files
source_file('evo_agent.py', 7872, 'Core agent: think() pipeline, tool loop, 3-tier triage, gate enforcement').
source_file('evo_prompt.py', 1971, 'System prompt definition, injected at runtime').
source_file('evo_gate_breach_ledger.py', 1094, 'Gate breach detection system: 25+ gate types across 3 layers').
source_file('evo_context.py', 973, 'Context window manager: offload, archive, compression').
source_file('config.py', 107, 'Env-based config; provider-aware token/throttling').
source_file('tools/proof_solver.py', 0, 'prove_problem MATHS/formal stage controller').
source_file('tools/maths_solver.py', 0, 'maths_problem MATHS stage controller').
source_file('tools/lean_eval_solver.py', 0, 'solve_lean_eval_problem orchestrator').
source_file('run_manager.py', 0, 'RunManager: worker threads, event channels, replay buffers').

%% Architecture facts from CLAUDE.md
tier_system(3, ['MATHS', 'CODE', 'REASON']).
rigor_modes(['computational', 'derivational', 'proof', 'formal']).
rigor_mode_details('formal', 'MATHS sub-mode using prove_problem + Lean 4 verification').
gate_layers(['Layer1_per_tool', 'Layer1_5_mid_loop', 'Layer2_answer_time']).
gate_count(25).

%% Key observations about the run
observation('run_log_length', 6737, 'Run log is 6737 lines, 300KB').
observation('run_duration_hours', 1, 'Run started 2026-07-04T23:19:22').
observation('problem_type', 'combinatorial_geometry', 'A line in plane is sunny if not parallel to x-axis, y-axis, or x+y=0').
observation('triage_requested', 'MATHS', 'User requested MATHS tier with rigor=formal').
observation('math_analysis_lines', 5000, 'Agent spent ~5000 lines on pure mathematical analysis').

%% Gate system facts
gate_breach_type('G3_Sorry', 'tool_result', 'Lean code contains sorry or admit placeholders').
gate_breach_type('G10_Assumptions', 'assistant_message', 'Missing assumption-dependence classification').
gate_breach_type('G16_CodePrologProxy', 'workflow_state', 'CODE-tier requires prolog_exec proxy model').

%% Workflow state machine for formal path
workflow_step('formal_path', 'prove_problem_start', ['required']).
workflow_step('formal_path', 'statement_skeleton', ['prove_problem_start', 'lean4_probe_success']).
workflow_step('formal_path', 'frontier_plan', ['statement_skeleton']).
workflow_step('formal_path', 'verify_frontier_lemma', ['frontier_plan']).
workflow_step('formal_path', 'verify_final', ['verify_frontier_lemma']).

workflow_step('fallback_path', 'formal_abandoned', ['prove_problem_start']).
workflow_step('fallback_path', 'maths_fallback_start', ['formal_abandoned']).
workflow_step('fallback_path', 'maths_model', ['maths_fallback_start']).
workflow_step('fallback_path', 'maths_derive', ['maths_model']).
workflow_step('fallback_path', 'maths_verify_final', ['maths_derive']).

%% Observed agent behavior in the run
agent_action('t0', 'start_math_analysis', 'Agent begins analyzing the sunny lines problem mathematically').
agent_action('t1', 'compute_examples', 'Agent computes examples for n=3,4,5,6,7').
agent_action('t2', 'identify_pattern', 'Agent identifies k ∈ {0,1,3} pattern').
agent_action('t3', 'attempt_formal_workflow', 'Agent calls prove_problem stage=start').
agent_action('t4', 'attempt_formal_abandon', 'Agent calls prove_problem stage=formal_abandoned').
agent_action('t5', 'attempt_fallback', 'Agent calls maths_problem stage=fallback_start with complexity=proof').
agent_action('t6', 'create_lean_skeleton', 'Agent creates Lean 4 skeleton and runs lean4_probe').
agent_action('t7', 'try_verify_final', 'Agent calls maths_problem stage=verify_final with evidence').

%% The observed loop problem
gate_state_transition(
    'formal_requirement_loop',
    [
        'agent_provides_math_proof',
        'gate_enforces_formal_rigor',
        'agent_creates_lean_skeleton',
        'gate_accepts_skeleton',
        'agent_tries_verify_final',
        'gate_enforces_formal_rigor_again'
    ]
).

%% CRITICAL BUG: formal_abandoned does NOT reset the rigor level
%% The gate system still sees the original "complexity=formal" setting
%% and keeps requiring Lean verification even after the fallback path is invoked
bug('circular_formal_fallback', 
    'formal_abandoned does not reset rigor level',
    'Gate system persists the original "formal" complexity setting even after formal_abandoned + fallback_start with complexity=proof').
bug('infinite_gate_loop',
    'Fallback path creates infinite meta-loop',
    'Agent provides proof evidence -> gate says formal required -> agent creates Lean skeleton -> gate accepts -> agent tries verify_final -> gate says formal required -> infinite loop').
bug('excessive_manual_reasoning',
    '5000 lines of manual chain-of-thought on pure math',
    'Agent should have used python_exec/sympy_exec/tools for exploration instead of 5000 lines of manual CoT reasoning').

%% Rules
conclusion('formal_fallback_path_has_circular_dependency') :-
    bug('circular_formal_fallback', _, _),
    bug('infinite_gate_loop', _, _).

conclusion('maths_formal_gate_does_not_allow_clean_escape') :-
    workflow_step('fallback_path', 'maths_verify_final', _),
    gate_state_transition('formal_requirement_loop', _).

conclusion('agent_overuses_manual_reasoning') :-
    observation('math_analysis_lines', Lines, _),
    Lines > 4000.

conclusion('tier_gate_persistence_causes_loop') :-
    bug('circular_formal_fallback', _, _),
    gate_state_transition('formal_requirement_loop', [_, gate_enforces_formal_rigor | _]).

%% Assumptions
assumption(log_complete, 'The run log accurately captures all agent actions and system responses up to 5000 lines').
assumption(codebase_analysis_representative, 'The codebase files examined (evo_agent.py, evo_prompt.py, evo_gate_breach_ledger.py, config.py, CLAUDE.md) are representative of the architecture').
assumption(gate_behavior_inferred, 'The gate behavior described is inferred from log output patterns and codebase structure').
assumption(run_not_completed, 'The run may not have completed; the log was still in progress at 5000+ lines').

%% --- Activate assumptions ---
activate :-
    forall(assumption(A, _),
           (\+ active_assumption(A) ->
               assertz(active_assumption(A))
           ; true)).

%% --- Main execution ---
main :-
    activate,
    write('=== CODE Tier - Prolog Reasoning Ledger ==='), nl, nl,
    write('Source files identified:'), nl,
    forall(source_file(Path, Lines, Desc),
           format('  ~w (~w lines) - ~w~n', [Path, Lines, Desc])), nl,
    write('Derived conclusions:'), nl,
    findall(C, conclusion(C), Conclusions),
    (Conclusions = [] ->
        write('  No conclusions derived'), nl
    ;
        forall(member(C, Conclusions),
               (prove(conclusion(C), Proof),
                format('  ~w~n    proof: ~w~n', [C, Proof])))
    ), nl,
    (inconsistent ->
        write('KB IS INCONSISTENT'), nl
    ;
        write('KB IS CONSISTENT'), nl
    ), nl,
    write('Assumption dependence test:'), nl,
    forall(conclusion(C),
           forall(active_assumption(A),
                  (retract(active_assumption(A)),
                   (prove(conclusion(C), _) ->
                       write('  ROBUST without '), write(A), nl
                   ;
                       write('  DEPENDS on '), write(A), nl
                   ),
                   assertz(active_assumption(A))))), nl,
    write('Bug report summary:'), nl,
    forall(bug(ID, Desc, Detail),
           format('  ~w: ~w~n    ~w~n', [ID, Desc, Detail])), nl,
    write('Gate state transition (loop pattern):'), nl,
    gate_state_transition(Name, Steps),
    forall(nth0(I, Steps, Step),
           format('  step ~w: ~w~n', [I, Step])), nl,
    write('=== End Reasoning Ledger ==='), nl.

    1: """Run management for EVO Web — owns agent worker threads and event channels.
    2: 
    3: Decouples run lifecycle from HTTP request handlers.  Each run has:
    4:   - A threaded worker (same as the existing ask_evo worker)
    5:   - A thread-safe queue bridging the worker thread to the WebSocket handler
    6:   - A replay buffer for reconnection catch-up
    7:   - A stop event for user-initiated cancellation
    8: """
    9: 
   10: import asyncio
   11: import itertools
   12: import json
   13: import queue
   14: import threading
   15: import time
   16: import traceback
   17: from collections import deque
   18: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   19: from datetime import datetime
   20: 
   21: 
   22: class RunStopped(Exception):
   23:     """Raised inside put() when the user has requested a stop."""
   24: 
   25: 
   26: @dataclass
   27: class Run:
   28:     run_id: str
   29:     channel: queue.Queue = field(default_factory=queue.Queue)  # thread-safe, worker → WS
   30:     events: deque = field(default_factory=lambda: deque(maxlen=2000))
   31:     stop: threading.Event = field(default_factory=threading.Event)
   32:     status: str = "running"
   33:     result_text: str | None = None
   34:     last_seq: int = 0
   35:     created_at: float = field(default_factory=time.monotonic)
   36: 
   37:     def append_event(self, seq: int, event_type: str, text: str | None) -> dict:
   38:         evt: dict = {"seq": seq, "type": event_type, "text": text or ""}
   39:         self.events.append(evt)
   40:         if seq > self.last_seq:
   41:             self.last_seq = seq
   42:         return evt
   43: 
   44: 
   45: class RunManager:
   46:     """Singleton that owns all active runs and their worker threads."""
   47: 
   48:     def __init__(self, max_age_seconds: int = 86400):
   49:         self._runs: dict[str, Run] = {}
   50:         self._lock = threading.Lock()
   51:         self._max_age = max_age_seconds
   52: 
   53:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   54:     # Public API (called from FastAPI routes)
   55:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   56: 
   57:     def create(
   58:         self,
   59:         query: str,
   60:         conversation_id: str,
   61:         username: str,
   62:         *,
   63:         conversations,     # ConversationStore (avoids circular import)
   64:         log_syncer,         # LogSyncer | None
   65:         verbose: bool = False,
   66:     ) -> str:
   67:         """Spawn a worker thread and return the run_id."""
   68:         from run_evo_web_login import TraceLogger  # deferred to break circular import
   69: 
   70:         trace = TraceLogger(query)
   71:         run_id = trace.run_id
   72:         run = Run(run_id=run_id)
   73:         with self._lock:
   74:             self._runs[run_id] = run
   75: 
   76:         thread = threading.Thread(
   77:             target=self._worker,
   78:             args=(run, query, conversation_id, username, trace, conversations, log_syncer, verbose),
   79:             daemon=True,
   80:         )
   81:         thread.start()
   82:         return run_id
   83: 
   84:     def get(self, run_id: str) -> Run | None:
   85:         with self._lock:
   86:             return self._runs.get(run_id)
   87: 
   88:     def stop(self, run_id: str) -> bool:
   89:         """Signal a run to stop.  Returns True if the run was found."""
   90:         with self._lock:
   91:             run = self._runs.get(run_id)
   92:         if run is None:
   93:             return False
   94:         run.stop.set()
   95:         return True
   96: 
   97:     def cleanup(self) -> int:
   98:         """Remove runs finished longer than _max_age seconds ago.  Returns count removed."""
   99:         now = time.monotonic()
  100:         removed = 0

    1: """Progressive gate-breach ledger for mid-loop EVO gate monitoring.
    2: 
    3: Mirrors the EvidenceLedger pattern (progressive extraction during tool
    4: execution) but tracks gate-policy breaches.  Breaches are detected as they
    5: happen — no waiting until the final response.  The ledger can inject
    6: corrective feedback immediately and persists breaches across tool-loop
    7: iterations within a turn.
    8: 
    9: Breaches are cleared only after the final response is produced.
   10: """
   11: 
   12: from __future__ import annotations
   13: 
   14: import re
   15: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   16: from typing import Literal
   17: 
   18: 
   19: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   20: # Data structures
   21: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   22: 
   23: DetectionPoint = Literal[
   24:     "tool_result",
   25:     "assistant_message",
   26:     "workflow_state",
   27: ]
   28: 
   29: 
   30: @dataclass
   31: class BreachEntry:
   32:     """A single gate breach detected during the tool-calling loop."""
   33: 
   34:     gate_name: str              # e.g. "G3:Sorry", "G9:Consistency"
   35:     iteration: int              # tool-loop iteration when first detected
   36:     detection_point: DetectionPoint
   37:     context: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
   38:     corrected: bool = False
   39:     correction_iteration: int | None = None
   40:     correction_attempts: int = 0
   41:     # Timestamps / ordinal for ordering
   42:     sequence: int = 0           # global sequence number within the turn
   43: 
   44: 
   45: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   46: # GateBreachLedger
   47: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   48: 
   49: class GateBreachLedger:
   50:     """Progressive ledger for gate-policy breaches.
   51: 
   52:     Detects breaches at three points during the tool-calling loop:
   53:       1. After each tool result — inspects tool output for quality breaches
   54:       2. After each assistant message — inspects text for pattern breaches
   55:       3. After workflow-state updates — records workflow-step gaps
   56: 
   57:     Breaches persist across iterations within a turn and are only cleared
   58:     when ``clear_all()`` is called (after the final response).
   59:     """
   60: 
   61:     # Gate names that are considered *critical* — they trigger immediate
   62:     # correction rather than waiting for the accumulation threshold.
   63:     CRITICAL_GATES: tuple[str, ...] = (
   64:         "G0:Lean", "G0m:Maths", "G0b:ProofComplete",
   65:         "G3:Sorry", "G5:PrologFirst",
   66:         "G14:UnverifiedLean",
   67:         "G20:LemmaCoverage",
   68:         "G23:SequentialProof",
   69:         "G24:LemmaBudget",
   70:     )
   71: 
   72:     # Maximum correction attempts per individual breach before giving up.
   73:     MAX_CORRECTION_ATTEMPTS: int = 2
   74: 
   75:     # Number of active (uncorrected) breaches that trigger a summary nudge.
   76:     ACCUMULATION_THRESHOLD: int = 3
   77: 
   78:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   79:     # Lifecycle
   80:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   81: 
   82:     def __init__(self) -> None:
   83:         self._breaches: list[BreachEntry] = []
   84:         self._active: dict[str, BreachEntry] = {}   # gate_name -> entry
   85:         self._sequence: int = 0
   86:         self._turn: int = 0
   87:         # Per-turn counters
   88:         self._corrections_injected_this_turn: int = 0
   89:         # G23/G24: per-turn lemma tracking
   90:         self._verified_lemma_names: set[str] = set()
   91:         self._lemma_probe_counts: dict[str, int] = {}
   92:         self._fan_out_called: bool = False
   93: 
   94:     def reset_turn(self) -> None:
   95:         """Reset per-turn tracking state.  Called at the start of each think().
   96: 
   97:         Active breaches are cleared (they will be re-detected if still
   98:         present), but the full breach history is preserved for the
   99:         end-of-turn gate sweep to consult.
  100:         """
  101:         self._active.clear()
  102:         self._corrections_injected_this_turn = 0
  103:         self._verified_lemma_names.clear()
  104:         self._lemma_probe_counts.clear()
  105:         self._fan_out_called = False
  106:         self._turn += 1
  107: 
  108:     def clear_all(self) -> None:
  109:         """Fully clear all breach history.  Called after the final response."""
  110:         self._breaches.clear()
  111:         self._active.clear()
  112:         self._sequence = 0
  113:         self._corrections_injected_this_turn = 0
  114: 
  115:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
  116:     # Query helpers
  117:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
  118: 
  119:     @property
  120:     def active_breaches(self) -> dict[str, BreachEntry]:
  121:         return dict(self._active)
  122: 
  123:     @property
  124:     def active_count(self) -> int:
  125:         return len(self._active)
  126: 
  127:     @property
  128:     def total_breaches_this_turn(self) -> int:
  129:         return len(self._breaches)
  130: 
  131:     def is_breached(self, gate_prefix: str) -> bool:
  132:         """Check if any gate starting with *gate_prefix* is currently active."""
  133:         return any(k.startswith(gate_prefix) for k in self._active)
  134: 
  135:     def was_corrected(self, gate_prefix: str) -> bool:
  136:         """Check if any gate starting with *gate_prefix* was corrected this turn."""
  137:         return any(
  138:             e.gate_name.startswith(gate_prefix) and e.corrected
  139:             for e in self._breaches
  140:         )
  141: 
  142:     def get_active_names(self) -> list[str]:
  143:         return sorted(self._active.keys())
  144: 
  145:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
  146:     # Core inspection entry points
  147:     # ------------------------------------------------------------------
  148: 
  149:     def inspect_tool_result(
  150:         self,
  151:         tool_name: str,
  152:         args: dict,
  153:         result: dict,
  154:         tier: str,
  155:         iteration: int,
  156:         tool_calls_so_far: list[dict] | None = None,
  157:         workflow_state: dict | None = None,
  158:         strategy_fingerprints: dict[str, int] | None = None,
  159:     ) -> list[str]:
  160:         """Inspect a tool result for gate breaches.
  161: 
  162:         Returns list of gate names that were newly breached this call.
  163:         """
  164:         newly_breached: list[str] = []
  165:         code = args.get("code", "") or ""
  166:         output = result.get("output", "") or ""
  167:         success = result.get("success", True)
  168: 
  169:         tc_history = tool_calls_so_far or []
  170: 
  171:         if tool_name in ("lean4_exec", "lean4_probe"):
  172:             newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_lean_sorry(code, output, iteration))
  173:             if not success:
  174:                 newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_unverified_lean(output, iteration))
  175:             else:
  176:                 # G19: lemma verified but no integration plan
  177:                 newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_lemma_integration(
  178:                     code, output, iteration))
  179:             # G20: duplicate verification or easy-only bias
  180:             newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_lemma_coverage(
  181:                 code, output, iteration, workflow_state))
  182:             # G21: repeated strategy on failure
  183:             if not success:
  184:                 newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_stale_approach(
  185:                     code, iteration, strategy_fingerprints))
  186:             # G23: sequential proof (verified 2+ lemmas without fan_out)
  187:             newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_sequential_proof(
  188:                 code, output, tier, iteration, tc_history))
  189:             # G24: per-lemma attempt budget
  190:             newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_lemma_budget(
  191:                 code, tier, iteration))
  192: 
  193:         elif tool_name == "evo_subagent":
  194:             action = str(args.get("action", "") or "").strip().lower()
  195:             if action == "fan_out":
  196:                 self._fan_out_called = True
  197: 
  198:         elif tool_name == "prolog_exec":
  199:             newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_prolog_harness(code, output, iteration, tier))
  200:             newly_breached.extend(self._inspect_findall(code, output, iteration, tier))

  104:             return self._statement_skeleton(
  105:                 problem,
  106:                 theorem_statement,
  107:                 statement_source,
  108:                 skeleton_verification,
  109:                 namespace,
  110:                 theorem_name,
  111:                 expected_sorry_count,
  112:                 definitions,
  113:                 variant,
  114:             )
  115:         if stage == "frontier_plan":
  116:             return self._frontier_plan(
  117:                 problem,
  118:                 theorem_statement,
  119:                 main_goal,
  120:                 closing_lemmas,
  121:                 mathlib_missing,
  122:                 local_plan,
  123:                 variant,
  124:             )
  125:         if stage == "register_frontier_lemma":
  126:             return self._register_frontier_lemma(problem, name, statement, depends_on)
  127:         if stage == "precheck":
  128:             return self._precheck(problem, name, test_code, engine)
  129:         if stage == "verify_frontier_lemma":
  130:             return self._verify_frontier_lemma(problem, name, candidate_proof, lean_verification)
  131:         if stage == "block_frontier_lemma":
  132:             return self._block_frontier_lemma(problem, name, failed_lean_error, next_lemma)
  133:         if stage == "prove_ready":
  134:             return self._prove_ready(problem, theorem_statement)
  135:         if stage == "verify_final":
  136:             return self._verify_final(problem, theorem_statement, candidate_proof, lean_verification)
  137:         if stage == "save_incomplete":
  138:             return self._save_incomplete(problem, candidate_proof, failed_lean_error, next_lemma, confirm=confirm)
  139:         if stage == "formal_abandoned":
  140:             return self._formal_abandoned(problem, failed_lean_error or next_lemma)
  141:         if stage == "status":
  142:             return self._status()
  143:         return self._error(
  144:             "stage must be start, statement_skeleton, frontier_plan, register_frontier_lemma, "
  145:             "verify_frontier_lemma, block_frontier_lemma, prove_ready, "
  146:             "verify_final, save_incomplete, formal_abandoned, or status."
  147:         )
  148: 
  149:     def _start(self, problem: str, theorem_statement: str) -> dict:
  150:         if not problem and not theorem_statement:
  151:             return self._error("problem or theorem_statement is required.")
  152:         self.state = ProofSolveState(
  153:             problem=problem or theorem_statement[:120],
  154:             theorem_statement=theorem_statement,
  155:             started=True,
  156:         )
  157:         return {
  158:             "success": True,
  159:             "output": (
  160:                 "MATHS/formal workflow start\n"
  161:                 + "=" * 60 + "\n"
  162:                 f"Problem: {self.state.problem or '(unspecified)'}\n"
  163:                 f"Theorem: {self.state.theorem_statement or '(derive from prompt)'}\n\n"
  164:                 "Next required stage: create a statement-only Lean skeleton "
  165:                 "with the final theorem signature and expected sorry placeholder, "
  166:                 "run lean4_probe on that exact skeleton, then call "
  167:                 "prove_problem stage=statement_skeleton with statement_source "
  168:                 "and raw skeleton_verification output. Only after the skeleton "
  169:                 "is accepted may you call stage=frontier_plan."
  170:             ),
  171:             "error": "",
  172:             "state": self._state_dict(),
  173:         }
  174: 
  175:     def _statement_skeleton(
  176:         self,
  177:         problem: str,
  178:         theorem_statement: str,
  179:         statement_source: str,
  180:         skeleton_verification: str,
  181:         namespace: str,
  182:         theorem_name: str,
  183:         expected_sorry_count,
  184:         definitions,
  185:         variant: str,
  186:     ) -> dict:
  187:         if not self.state.started:
  188:             started = self._start(problem, theorem_statement)
  189:             if not started.get("success"):
  190:                 return started
  191:         if problem and self.state.problem and problem != self.state.problem:
  192:             return self._error(f"Workflow is active for {self.state.problem}, not {problem}.")
  193:         statement_source = statement_source or ""
  194:         if not statement_source.strip():
  195:             return self._error("statement_skeleton requires statement_source.")
  196:         if "import Mathlib" not in statement_source:
  197:             return self._error("statement_skeleton requires a self-contained Lean file importing Mathlib.")
  198:         if "namespace " not in statement_source:
  199:             return self._error("statement_skeleton requires an explicit namespace.")
  200:         theorem_names = re.findall(r"^\s*theorem\s+([A-Za-z0-9_'.]+)\b", statement_source, re.MULTILINE)
  201:         if len(theorem_names) != 1:
  202:             return self._error(
  203:                 f"statement_skeleton requires exactly one theorem target, found {len(theorem_names)}."
  204:             )
  205:         expected_count = self._int_value(expected_sorry_count, default=1)
  206:         actual_count = self._count_word_without_comments(statement_source, "sorry")
  207:         if actual_count != expected_count:
  208:             return self._error(
  209:                 f"statement_skeleton expected {expected_count} sorry placeholder(s), found {actual_count}."
  210:             )
  211:         if FORBIDDEN_SKELETON_RE.search(self._strip_line_comments(statement_source)):
  212:             return self._error("statement_skeleton contains admit/axiom/unsafe/native_decide.")
  213:         if theorem_name and theorem_names[0] != theorem_name:
  214:             return self._error(f"statement_skeleton does not define theorem {theorem_name}.")
  215:         if not self._lean_probe_ready(skeleton_verification):
  216:             return self._error(
  217:                 "statement_skeleton requires raw lean4_probe output with lean4_probe_exit_code(0) "
  218:                 "and status: lean4_probe_ready_with_sorry or status: lean4_probe_ready."
  219:             )
  220:         hash_error = self._probe_hash_error(statement_source, skeleton_verification)
  221:         if hash_error:
  222:             return self._error(hash_error)
  223: 
  224:         self.state.theorem_statement = theorem_statement or self.state.theorem_statement
  225:         self.state.statement_source = statement_source
  226:         self.state.statement_sha256 = hashlib.sha256(statement_source.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
  227:         self.state.namespace = (namespace or self._extract_namespace(statement_source)).strip()
  228:         self.state.theorem_name = (theorem_name or self._extract_theorem_name(statement_source)).strip()
  229:         self.state.expected_sorry_count = expected_count
  230:         self.state.definitions = self._string_list(definitions) or self._extract_definitions(statement_source)
  231:         self.state.variant = (variant or self.state.variant or "main").strip()
  232:         self.state.skeleton_verified = True
  233:         self.state.last_error = ""
  234:         return {
  235:             "success": True,
  236:             "output": (
  237:                 "MATHS/formal statement skeleton accepted\n"
  238:                 f"namespace: {self.state.namespace or '(not parsed)'}\n"
  239:                 f"theorem_name: {self.state.theorem_name or '(not parsed)'}\n"
  240:                 f"variant: {self.state.variant}\n"
  241:                 f"statement_sha256: {self.state.statement_sha256}\n"
  242:                 f"expected_sorry_count: {self.state.expected_sorry_count}\n"
  243:                 f"definitions: {', '.join(self.state.definitions) if self.state.definitions else '(none)'}\n"
  244:                 "Next required stage: prove_problem stage=frontier_plan with "
  245:                 "named definitions, required closing lemmas, dependency plan, "
  246:                 "and local machinery plan."
  247:             ),
  248:             "error": "",
  249:             "state": self._state_dict(),
  250:         }
  251: 
  252:     def _frontier_plan(
  253:         self,
  254:         problem: str,
  255:         theorem_statement: str,
  256:         main_goal: str,
  257:         closing_lemmas,
  258:         mathlib_missing,
  259:         local_plan: str,
  260:         variant: str = "",
  261:     ) -> dict:
  262:         if not self.state.started:
  263:             started = self._start(problem, theorem_statement)
  264:             if not started.get("success"):
  265:                 return started
  266:         if not self.state.skeleton_verified:
  267:             if self._is_workspace_variant(variant or self.state.variant):
  268:                 self._accept_workspace_skeleton(variant or self.state.variant or "lean_eval")
  269:             else:
  270:                 return self._error("frontier_plan requires stage=statement_skeleton first.")
  271:         if problem and self.state.problem and problem != self.state.problem:
  272:             return self._error(f"Workflow is active for {self.state.problem}, not {problem}.")
  273:         main_goal = (main_goal or self.state.main_goal or theorem_statement or self.state.theorem_statement).strip()
  274:         required = self._string_list(closing_lemmas)
  275:         missing = self._string_list(mathlib_missing)
  276:         local_plan = (local_plan or "").strip()
  277:         if not main_goal:
  278:             return self._error("frontier_plan requires main_goal.")
  279:         if not required:
  280:             return self._error("frontier_plan requires at least one closing_lemma.")
  281:         if missing and not local_plan:
  282:             return self._error("frontier_plan with missing Mathlib lemmas requires local_plan.")
  283: 
  284:         self.state.main_goal = main_goal
  285:         self.state.required_closing_lemmas = required
  286:         self.state.missing_mathlib_lemmas = missing
  287:         self.state.local_machinery_plan = local_plan
  288:         for lemma in required:
  289:             self.state.frontier.setdefault(lemma, FrontierLemma(name=lemma))
  290:         for lemma in missing:
  291:             self.state.frontier.setdefault(lemma, FrontierLemma(name=lemma))
  292:         self.state.current_frontier_lemma = self._first_unverified_frontier()
  293:         self.state.next_lemma = self.state.current_frontier_lemma
  294:         self.state.last_error = ""
  295: 
  296:         missing_lines = [f"- {lemma}: not in Mathlib; create local lemma." for lemma in missing]
  297:         return {
  298:             "success": True,
  299:             "output": "\n".join([
  300:                 "MATHS/formal frontier plan registered",
  301:                 "=" * 60,
  302:                 f"main_goal: {self.state.main_goal}",
  303:                 "required_closing_lemmas:",
  304:                 *[f"- {lemma}" for lemma in required],
  305:                 "missing_mathlib_lemmas:",
  306:                 *(missing_lines or ["- (none declared)"]),
  307:                 f"local_machinery_plan: {self.state.local_machinery_plan or '(none)'}",
  308:                 "",
  309:                 "NEXT (DO THIS NOW): Workers run async and need time — spawn "
  310:                 "them IMMEDIATELY.  For ALL ready frontier lemmas, call "
  311:                 "evo_subagent fan_out in ONE call (or spawn for a single lemma). "
  312:                 "Do not read files, search mathlib, "
  313:                 "or do anything else first — workers need head start.",
  314:                 "",
  315:                 "Workers are FULL EvoAgents — they are as capable as you.  A "
  316:                 "worker CAN prove any lemma you can prove.  If lemmas are "
  317:                 "interdependent, pass verified dependency statements as context "
  318:                 "to each worker.  Never decide a lemma is \"too complex\" for "
  319:                 "a worker — that is false, and manual proving is your #1 "
  320:                 "anti-pattern.  fan_out all workers, then wait for results.",
  321:                 "",
  322:                 "Self-verify with lean4_exec, register via "
  323:                 "stage=verify_frontier_lemma. Max 4 concurrent. Follow the "
  324:                 "MATHS/formal frontier orchestration loop.",
  325:                 "",
  326:                 "To re-plan: call stage=frontier_plan again with an adjusted "
  327:                 "closing_lemmas list.  Verified lemmas are preserved; new lemma "
  328:                 "names are added; dropped lemmas will not block verify_final.",
  329:             ]),
  330:             "error": "",
  331:             "state": self._state_dict(),
  332:         }
  333: 
  334:     def _register_frontier_lemma(self, problem: str, name: str, statement: str, depends_on) -> dict:
  335:         if not self.state.started:
  336:             return self._error("Run stage=start before registering frontier lemmas.")
  337:         if problem and self.state.problem and problem != self.state.problem:
  338:             return self._error(f"Workflow is active for {self.state.problem}, not {problem}.")
  339:         name = self._safe_lemma_name(name)
  340:         if not name:
  341:             return self._error("register_frontier_lemma requires name.")
  342:         statement = (statement or "").strip()
  343:         if not statement:
  344:             return self._error("register_frontier_lemma requires statement.")
  345:         lemma = self.state.frontier.get(name) or FrontierLemma(name=name)
  346:         lemma.statement = statement
  347:         lemma.depends_on = self._string_list(depends_on)
  348:         lemma.status = "pending"
  349:         lemma.last_error = ""

  371:         """Return a structured precheck instruction.  The orchestrator does NOT

  373:         instruction the agent must follow before calling verify_frontier_lemma."""

  378:             return self._error("Run stage=start before precheck.")
  379:         if self.state.problem and problem != self.state.problem:
  380:             return self._error(f"Workflow active for {self.state.problem}, not {problem}.")
  381:         name = (name or self.state.current_frontier_lemma or "").strip()
  382:         if not name:

  384:         if not test_code or not test_code.strip():

  387:         if engine not in ("python", "z3"):
